“那辆大篷车，就是那小贩卖给您的吧？”他斜着眼问。

“是，”我简短地答。

“我猜，您这是回去告他吧？”他说，“嘿，那家伙可是个恶煞，这您得信我的。警长要给他上手铐，他一拳打青了警长的眼眶，下巴颏险些叫他打断。小个子里的打架好手！”

我自己那位勇敢的小斗士——我想着，骄傲得脸上发热。

回波特维戈的路仿佛没有尽头。我有点心慌，想起普拉特（Pratt）家采石场那伙流浪汉，可博克就坐在我身旁的车座上，我觉得再惊惶就太怯懦了。我们轻轻辘辘地碾过黑暗，穿行在墨黑松林的夹道间，头顶一线星光像一条缎带；随后驶上俯瞰水面的起伏沙丘。天上还有月亮，可我累得要死，孤单得要死，一心只想见到我的小红胡子。佩格也乏了，一步一步慢吞吞地走。待到望见铁道信号的红灯绿灯、知道波特维戈就在眼前时，准已是半夜了。

===Original===
I decided to camp where I was. I guided Peg into a field beside the road, hitched her to a fence, and took the dog into the van with me. I was too tired to undress. I fell into the bunk and drew the blankets over me. As I did so, something dropped down behind the bunk with a sharp rap. It was a forgotten corncob pipe of the Professor’s, blackened and sooty. I put it under my pillow, and fell asleep.

Monday, October seventh. If this were a novel about some charming, slender, pansy-eyed girl, how differently I would have to describe the feelings with which I woke the next morning. But these being only a few pages from the life of a fat, New England housewife, I must be candid. I woke feeling dull and sour. The day was gray and cool: faint shreds of mist sifting up from the Sound and a desolate mewing of seagulls in the air. I was unhappy, upset, and—yes—shy. Passionately I yearned to run to the Professor, to gather him into my arms, to be alone with him in Parnassus, creaking up some sunny byroad. But his words came back to me: I was nothing to him. What if he didn’t love me after all?

I walked across two fields, down to the beach where little waves were slapping against the shingle. I washed my face and hands in salt water. Then I went back to Parnassus and brewed some coffee with condensed milk. I gave Peg and Bock their breakfasts. Then I hitched Peg to the van again, and felt better. As I drove into the town I had to wait at the grade crossing while a wrecking train rumbled past, on its way back from Willdon. That meant that the line was clear again. I watched the grimy men on the cars, and shuddered to think what they had been doing.

The Vigor county jail lies about a mile out of the town, an ugly, gray stone barracks with a high, spiked wall about it. I was thankful that it was still fairly early in the morning, and I drove through the streets without seeing anyone I knew. Finally I reached the gate in the prison wall. Here some kind of a keeper barred my way. “Can’t get in, lady,” he said. “Yesterday was visitors’ day. No more visitors till next month.”

===Chinese===
我决计就地露宿。我把佩格领进路旁一块地，拴在栅栏上，带着狗钻进车厢。我累得连衣服都懒得脱，一头栽进铺位，拉过毯子盖上。就在这时，有什么东西从铺位后头掉下来，当啷一响。是教授落下的一支玉米芯烟斗，熏得乌黑。我把它塞在枕头底下，睡着了。

十月七号，礼拜一。倘若这是写某个苗条可人、生着一双紫罗兰色眼睛的姑娘的小说，我描写次日早晨醒来时的心情，就得换一副全然不同的笔墨了。可这不过是一个新英格兰胖主妇生活里的几页，我只得实说。我醒来时又闷又酸。天色灰暗清冷：长岛海峡上筛起一缕缕薄雾，空中海鸥的啼声凄凉。我不快活，心烦意乱，而且——是的——害羞。我热烈地渴望奔到教授跟前，把他搂进怀里，同他单独待在帕纳索斯号上，吱吱嘎嘎驶上哪条洒满阳光的小路。可他的话又回到耳边：我于他什么也不是。万一他到底不爱我呢？

我穿过两块田，走到滩头，小小的浪花正拍打着卵石。我用咸水洗了脸和手。然后回到帕纳索斯号，就着炼乳煮了些咖啡。我喂了佩格和博克早饭。再给佩格套上车时，我心里好受了些。驶进镇里时，我在铁道平交道口停下来，等一列救援列车辘辘驶过——它是从威尔登回来的。这就是说，线路又通了。我望着车皮上那些满身油污的人，一想到他们一直在干的营生，不禁发抖。

维格县监狱在镇外约一英里处，一座难看的灰色石头营房，四周围着高高的尖刺墙。幸好天还早，我穿过街市没撞见一个熟人。末了我到了监狱墙下的大门前。一个什么看门的拦住我。“进不去，太太，”他说，“昨天是探视日。下个月之前不接待探视了。”

===Original===
“I *must* get in,” I said. “You’ve got a man in there on a false charge.”

“So they all say,” he retorted, calmly, and spat halfway across the road. “You wouldn’t believe any of our boarders had a right to be here if you could hear their friends talk.”

I showed him Governor Stafford’s card. He was rather impressed by this, and retired into a sentry box in the wall—to telephone, I suppose.

Presently he came back.

“The sheriff says he’ll see you, ma’am. But you’ll have to leave this here dynamite caboose behind.” He unlocked a little door in the immense iron gate, and turned me over to another man inside. “Take this here lady to the sheriff,” he said.

===Chinese===
“我非进去不可，”我说，“你们里头关着一个人，罪名是冤枉的。”

“他们个个都这么说，”他不动声色地顶了一句，一口唾沫吐出半条马路。“要是听他们朋友的口气，您还当咱们这儿的房客，没一个有资格蹲在这儿呢。”

我给他看斯塔福德州长的名片。这叫他肃然了几分，退进墙上的一个岗亭里——我猜是打电话去了。

不多时他回来了。

“治安官说他愿意见您，太太。不过您得把您这节火药车厢留在后头。”他在巨大的铁门上开了一扇小门，把我交给里头另一个男人。“带这位太太去见治安官，”他说。

===Original===
Some of Vigor county’s prisoners must have learned to be pretty good gardeners, for certainly the grounds were in good condition. The grass was green and trimly mowed; there were conventional beds of flowers in very ugly shapes; in the distance I saw a gang of men in striped overalls mending a roadway. The guide led me to an attractive cottage to one side of the main building. There were two children playing outside, and I remember thinking that within the walls of a jail was surely a queer place to bring up youngsters.

But I had other things to think about. I looked up at that grim, gray building. Behind one of those little barred windows was the Professor. I should have been angry at Andrew, but somehow it all seemed a kind of dream. Then I was taken into the hallway of the sheriff’s cottage and in a minute I was talking to a big, bull-necked man with a political moustache.

“You have a prisoner here called Roger Mifflin?” I said.

“My dear Madam, I don’t keep a list of all our inmates in my head. If you will come to the office we will look up the records.”

I showed him the Governor’s card. He took it and kept looking at it as though he expected to see the message written there change or fade away. We walked across a strip of lawn to the prison building. There, in a big bare office, he ran over a card index.

===Chinese===
维格县的一些囚犯，想必都学成了好园丁，因为监区的场院确实收拾得不错。草地青青，修剪整齐；花圃齐整，形状却俗气得很；远处我望见一队穿条纹工装裤的人在修路。向导领我到主楼侧边一座惹眼的小屋。门外有两个孩子玩耍，我记得我当时还想，把娃娃养育在监狱的高墙之内，真是个古怪的地方。

可我有别的心事要想。我仰头望那森冷的灰楼。那些装着铁栏的小窗后面某一扇，就是教授。我本该生安德鲁的气，可不知怎么，这一切恍如一场梦。随后我被领进治安官小屋的门厅，转眼间就同一个大块头说起话来——他脖颈粗壮如牛，嘴唇上蓄着一撇政客式的小胡子。

“你们这儿关着一个叫罗杰·米夫林的犯人？”我说。

“亲爱的夫人，全体在押人犯的名册，我可记不到脑子里。您随我到办公室去，咱们查查档案。”

我给他看州长的名片。他接过去，翻来覆去地端详，仿佛指望上面写的字会变样或褪掉似的。我们穿过一片草坪，走进监狱大楼。在那间空荡荡的大办公室里，他翻检起一柜卡片索引。

===Original===
“Here we are,” he said. “Roger Mifflin; age, 41; face, oval; complexion, florid; hair, red but not much of it; height, 64 inches; weight, stripped, 120; birthmark—”

“Never mind,” I said. “That’s the man. What’s he here for?”

“He’s held in default of bail, pending trial. The charge is attempt to defraud one Helen McGill, spinster, age—”

“Rubbish!” I said. “I’m Helen McGill, and the man made no attempt to defraud me.”

“The charge was entered and warrant applied for by your brother, Andrew McGill, acting on your behalf.”

===Chinese===
“有了，”他说，“罗杰·米夫林：年龄 41；脸型，卵圆；面色，红润；头发，红色，所剩无几；身高 64 英寸；体重，净重 120；胎记——”

“不必念了，”我说，“正是此人。他为什么蹲在这儿？”

“他取保未获，候审羁押。罪名是，企图诈骗一名海伦·麦吉尔，未婚女子，年龄——”

“胡说八道！”我说，“我就是海伦·麦吉尔，这人从没企图诈骗我。”

“罪名是你哥哥安德鲁·麦吉尔报立、申请拘票的，说是代你行事。”

===Original===
“I never authorized Andrew to act on my behalf.”

“Then do you withdraw the charge?”

“By all means,” I said. “I’ve a great mind to enter a countercharge against Andrew and have *him* arrested.”

“This is all very irregular,” said the sheriff, “but if the prisoner is known to the Governor, I suppose there is no alternative. I cannot annul the warrant without some recognizance. According to the laws of this state the next of kin must stand surety for the prisoner’s good behaviour after release. There is no next of kin …”

===Chinese===
“我从来没委托安德鲁代我行事。”

“那么你撤销控告？”

“当然撤销，”我说，“我倒很想反过来告安德鲁一状，让他也尝尝蹲班房的滋味。”

“这一切全不合规矩，”治安官说，“不过犯人既然是州长认识的，我想也别无办法。没有相当的保结，我无法撤销拘票。按本州法律，犯人获释后，须有其最近亲属为其善行作保。他没有最近亲属……”

===Original===
“Surely there is!” I said. “I am the prisoner’s next of kin.”

“What do you mean?” he said. “In what relationship do you stand to this Roger Mifflin?”

“I intend to marry him just as soon as I can get him away from here.”

He burst into a roar of laughter. “I guess there’s no stopping you,” he said. He pinned the Governor’s card to a blue paper on the desk, and began filling in some blanks.

“Well, Miss McGill,” he went on, “don’t take away more than one of my prisoners or I’ll lose my job. The turnkey will take you up to the cell. I’m exceedingly sorry: you can see that the mistake was none of our fault. Tell the Governor that, will you, when you see him?”

===Chinese===
“怎么会没有！”我说，“我就是犯人的最近亲属。”

“你这是什么意思？”他说，“你同这位罗杰·米夫林是什么关系？”

“我只等把他从这里弄出去，就嫁给他。”

他放声大笑。“看来没人拦得住你，”他说。他把州长的名片别在桌上一张蓝纸上，动手填写起几栏空格。

“那么，麦吉尔小姐，”他接着说，“别带走我一个以上的犯人，不然我这饭碗就砸了。狱吏会带你到牢房去。万分抱歉：您看得出，这个差错不是我们的过失。见到州长的时候，替我说一声，好吗？”

===Original===
I followed the attendant up two flights of bare, stone stairs, and down a long, whitewashed corridor. It was a gruesome place; rows and rows of heavy doors with little, barred windows. I noticed that each door had a combination knob, like a safe. My knees felt awfully shaky.

But it wasn’t really so heart-throbby as I had expected. The jailer stopped at the end of a long passageway. He spun the clicking dial, while I waited in a kind of horror. I think I expected to see the Professor with shaved head (they couldn’t shave much off his head, poor lamb!) and striped canvas suit, and a ball and chain on his ankle.

===Chinese===
我跟着看守登上两段光秃秃的石楼梯，走进一条长长的白灰走廊。那是个阴森的地方；一排又一排沉重的铁门，门上是小小的铁栏窗。我注意到每扇门上都有一个转字旋钮，跟保险柜一样。我的膝盖直打晃。

其实并不像我想的那样惊心动魄。狱卒在长廊尽头停下，拨着咔嗒作响的字盘，我提着一颗心等着。我想我原以为会看见教授剃了光头（他头上也没多少可剃的了，可怜的人！）、穿着条纹帆布囚衣、脚踝上拴着铁球铁链。

===Original===
The door swung open heavily. There was a narrow, clean little room with a low camp bed, and under the barred window a table strewn with sheets of paper. It was the Professor in his own clothes, writing busily, with his back toward me. Perhaps he thought it was only an attendant with food, or perhaps he didn’t even hear the interruption. I could hear his pen running busily. I might have known you never would get any heroics out of that man! Trust him to make the best of it!

“Lemon sole and a glass of sherry, please, James,” said the Professor over his shoulder, and the warder, who evidently had joked with him before, broke into a cackle of laughter.

“A lady to see yer Lordship,” he said.

The Professor turned round. His face went quite white. For the first time in my experience of him he seemed to be at a loss for speech.

“Miss—Miss McGill,” he stammered. “You *are* the good Samaritan. I’m doing the John Bunyan act, see? Writing in prison. I’ve really started my book at last. And I find the fellows here know nothing whatever about literature. There isn’t even a library in the place.”

===Chinese===
门沉重地荡开。一间窄小而洁净的屋子，一张低矮的行军床，铁栏窗下一张桌子，摊满纸页。正是教授，穿着他自己的衣服，背对着我，正奋笔疾书。也许他只当是看守送饭来了，也许他压根没听见有人进来。我听得见他的笔在纸上沙沙地跑。我早该想到，那个人身上你永远榨不出半点悲情戏码！他总能随遇而安！

“柠檬龙利鱼，再来一杯雪利酒，麻烦了，詹姆斯，”教授头也不回地说。狱卒显然早跟他逗过乐子，登时咯咯笑出声来。

“有位太太来见您大人。”他说。

教授转过身。他的脸刷地白了。据我对他的全部了解，这是头一回，他像是不知道说什么好。

“麦——麦吉尔小姐，”他结结巴巴地说，“您真是位好撒玛利亚人。我这可是在学约翰·班扬（John Bunyan，他在狱中写出了《天路历程》）的样儿，瞧见没有？坐牢写书。我的书总算真正开了头。我还发现，这儿这帮家伙对文学一窍不通。这地方连个图书室都没有。”

===Original===
For the life of me, I couldn’t utter the tenderness in my heart with that gorilla of a jailer standing behind us.

Somehow we made our way downstairs, after the Professor had gathered together the sheets of his manuscript. It had already reached formidable proportions, as he had written fifty pages in the thirty-six hours he had been in prison. In the office we had to sign some papers. The sheriff was very apologetic to Mifflin, and offered to take him back to town in his car, but I explained that Parnassus was waiting at the gate. The Professor’s eyes brightened when he heard that, but I had to hurry him away from an argument about putting good books in prisons. The sheriff walked with us to the gate and there shook hands again.

Peg whickered as we came up to her, and the Professor patted her soft nose. Bock tugged at his chain in a frenzy of joy. At last we were alone.

===Chinese===
那个大猩猩似的狱卒站在我们背后，我心里纵有万种柔情，也一个字吐不出来。

教授收拢他的手稿，我们好歹下了楼。稿子已经蔚为可观——他蹲了三十六个钟头，写出了五十页。在办公室里我们签了几份文书。治安官对米夫林百般致歉，还提出用自己的汽车送他回镇，可我说明帕纳索斯号就在大门口等着。教授一听，眼睛都亮了，可我不得不赶紧拽他走，不然他就要跟人家辩论起监狱里该备好书的问题了。治安官送我们到大门口，又握了一回手。

我们走近时，佩格打响鼻招呼，教授拍了拍她柔软的鼻子。博克拉着链子，欢喜得发狂。我们总算单独在一起了。

## XV / 十五

===Original===
I never knew just how it happened. Instead of driving back through Port Vigor, we turned into a side road leading up over the hill and across the heath where the air came fresh and sweet from the sea. The Professor sat very silent, looking about him. There was a grove of birches on the hill, and the sunlight played upon their satin boles.

“It feels good to be out again,” he said calmly. “The Sage cannot be so keen a lover of open air as his books would indicate, or he wouldn’t be so ready to clap a man into quod. Perhaps I owe him another punch on the nose for that.”

“Oh, Roger,” I said—and I’m afraid my voice was trembly—“I’m *sorry*. I’m *sorry*.”

Not very eloquent, was it? And then, somehow or other, his arm was around me.

===Chinese===
这到底是怎么发生的，我一直没弄明白。我们没有折回波特维戈，而是拐上一条岔路，翻过山冈，穿过荒原，海上来的空气又新鲜又甜润。教授坐在那里，非常安静，四下打量。山冈上有一片桦树林，阳光在她们缎面似的树干上嬉戏。

“又出来了，感觉真好，”他平静地说。“那位圣哲对户外空气的热爱，想必不及他书里写的那样热烈，不然他不会这么随手就把人往大牢里一关。为这个，没准我还欠他鼻子上一拳呢。”

“哦，罗杰，”我说——只怕我的声音发颤——“对不起。真对不起。”

算不上多有口才，是吧？随后，不知怎么一来，他的胳膊已经搂住了我。

===Original===
“Helen,” he said. “Will you marry me? I’m not rich, but I’ve saved up enough to live on. We’ll always have Parnassus, and this winter we’ll go and live in Brooklyn and write the book. And we’ll travel around with Peg, and preach the love of books and the love of human beings. Helen—you’re just what I need, God bless you. Will you come with me and make me the happiest bookseller in the world?”

Peg must have been astonished at the length of time she had for cropping the grass, undisturbed. I know that Roger and I sat careless of time. And when he told me that ever since our first afternoon together he had determined to have me, sooner or later, I was the proudest woman in New England. I told Roger about the ghastly wreck, and my agony of apprehension. I think it was the wreck that made us both feel inclined to forgive Andrew.

We had a light luncheon together there on the dunes above the Sound. By taking a shortcut over the ridge we struck into the Shelby road without going down into Port Vigor again. Peg pulled us along toward Greenbriar, and we talked as we went.

===Chinese===
“海伦，”他说，“你肯嫁给我吗？我不阔绰，可我攒下的钱也够过日子了。我们永远有帕纳索斯号，今年冬天我们去布鲁克林住，把书写出来。我们带着佩格四处游历，传布对书的爱、对人的爱。海伦——你正是我需要的人，愿上帝保佑你。你肯跟我走，让我做世上最幸福的卖书人吗？”

佩格准是纳闷，今天怎么由着她安安稳稳啃了那么久的草。我知道，罗杰和我坐着，把时辰全忘了。当他告诉我，打我们头一个下午在一起起，他就打定了主意，迟早要娶我，我成了新英格兰最骄傲的女人。我把那场可怕的车祸、我那阵提心吊胆的煎熬，讲给罗杰听。我想，正是那场车祸，叫我们俩都愿意原谅安德鲁了。

我们就在长岛海峡上头的沙丘上，共进了一顿简便的午餐。抄捷径翻过山脊，我们便插上了谢尔比的大道，不必再下波特维戈去。佩格拉着我们朝格林布赖尔走，我们一路说着话。

===Original===
Perhaps the best of it was that a cold drizzle of rain began to fall as we moved along the hill road. The Professor—as I still call him, by force of habit—curtained in the front of the van with a rubber sheet. Bock hopped up and curled himself aginst his master’s leg. Roger got out his corncob pipe, and I sat close to him. In the gathering gloom we plodded along, as happy a trio—or quartet, if you include fat, cheery old Peg—as any on this planet. Summer was over, and we were no longer young, but there were great things before us. I listened to the drip of the rain, and the steady creak of Parnassus on her axles. I thought of my “anthology” of loaves of bread and vowed to bake a million more if Roger wanted me to. It was after supper time when we got to Greenbriar. Roger had suggested that we take a shorter road that would have brought us through to Redfield sooner, but I begged him to go by way of Shelby and Greenbriar, just as we had come before. I did not tell him why I wanted this. And when finally we came to a halt in front of Kirby’s store at the crossroads it was raining heavily and we were ready for a rest.

===Chinese===
也许最美妙的是，我们沿山路行进时，一阵凉丝丝的毛毛雨飘了下来。教授——我至今还这么叫他，习惯成自然——拿一块胶布把车厢前脸遮上。博克跳上车，蜷在主人腿边。罗杰取出他的玉米芯烟斗，我紧挨着他坐。渐浓的暮色里，我们一路跋涉，是这颗星球上再快活不过的三口——算上快活肥胖的老佩格，就是四口。夏天过去了，我们也不再年轻，可前程上有的是大事。我听着雨的滴答，听着帕纳索斯号车轴上均匀的吱嘎。我想起我那“面包选集”，起了誓：只要罗杰要，我就再烤上一百万个。到格林布赖尔时，已过了晚饭时分。罗杰本提议抄一条近道，能更快赶到雷德菲尔德，可我求他走谢尔比和格林布赖尔那条路，就照我们来时的走法。我没告诉他我为什么想这样。等我们终于停在十字路口柯比（Kirby）家的铺子前时，雨已经下大了，我们也正该歇息了。

===Original===
“Well, sweetheart,” said Roger, “shall we go and see what sort of rooms the hotel has?”

“I can think of something better than that,” said I. “Let’s go up to Mr. Kane and have him marry us. Then we can get back to Sabine Farm afterward, and give Andrew a surprise.”

“By the bones of Hymen!” said Roger. “You’re right!”

It must have been ten o’clock when we turned in at the red gate of Sabine Farm. The rain had stopped, but the wheels sloshed through mud and water at every turn. The light was burning in the sitting room, and through the window I could see Andrew bent over his work table. We climbed out, stiff and sore from the long ride. I saw Roger’s face set in a comical blend of sternness and humour.

===Chinese===
“怎么样，心肝儿，”罗杰说，“要不要去看看旅馆的房间像什么样？”

“我想得出更好的主意，”我说，“咱们这就去找凯恩牧师（Mr. Kane），请他给我们主婚。回头再回萨宾农场，给安德鲁一个惊喜。”

“凭许门神的骸骨起誓！”罗杰说，“你说得对！”

我们拐进萨宾农场的红漆栅门时，准已十点。雨停了，可车轮每转一圈，都溅起泥水。起居室里亮着灯，隔着窗，我看见安德鲁伏在他的工作台上。我们爬下车，长途颠簸，浑身又僵又疼。我看见罗杰的脸板出一副严峻与诙谐古怪掺半的神情。

===Original===
“Well, here goes to surprise the Sage!” he whispered.

We picked our way between puddles and rapped on the door. Andrew appeared, carrying the lamp in one hand. When he saw us he grunted.

“Let me introduce my wife,” said Roger.

“Well, I’ll be damned,” said Andrew.

===Chinese===
“好，这就去吓圣哲一跳！”他小声说。

我们拣着干地绕过水洼，叩响了房门。安德鲁一手端着灯出来。一见我们，他哼了一声。

“请容我介绍我的妻子。”罗杰说。

“嘿，真见鬼。”安德鲁说。

===Original===
But Andrew isn’t quite so black as I’ve painted him. When he’s once convinced of the error of his ways, he is almost pathetically eager to make up. I remember only one remark in the subsequent conversation, because I was so appalled by the state of everything at Sabine Farm that I immediately set about putting the house to rights. The two men, however, as soon as Parnassus was housed in the barn and the animals under cover, sat down by the stove to talk things over.

“I tell you what,” said Andrew—“do whatever you like with your wife; she’s too much for me. But I’d like to buy that Parnassus.”

“Not on your life!” said the Professor.

===Chinese===
不过安德鲁并不像我抹得那么黑。一旦认了自己理亏，他那赔补的热切劲儿，几乎叫人可怜。后来的谈话我只记得一句，因为萨宾农场里那副天下大乱的景象叫我心惊，我立刻动手收拾屋子去了。倒是那两个男人，等帕纳索斯号进了谷仓、牲口上了棚，便在炉边坐下，把事情慢慢聊开了。

“我跟你说，”安德鲁说——“你媳妇随你怎么使唤，我是招架不住了。不过我倒想买下那辆帕纳索斯号。”

“想都别想！”教授说。
